ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e2881	Mr. Federal Minister.

It is necessary to counter-regulatory growth and the easy-to-use receipt of joint arrests or guarantees. This is what we are trying to do with the consumer protection law recently adopted in the Committee on Justice. There are certain information obligations of the creditor to the creditor. In the event of the loss of liability, there is a right of judicial measure, which, under certain conditions, can also completely excuse the members of the liability. We also have certain objections from the practice that the remaining debt waiver procedure can be carried out at all, if it comes to liability and the financial possibilities of the participant are limited, by providing in this Company Reorganisation Act that the opening of the insolvency proceedings no longer requires majority of creditors. Within the procedure given only to the creditor, there are also amortizations already contained in the private bankruptcy rule, namely that if the creditor does not reach the final rate of 10 per cent, the court may also issue a debt in respect of benefits below 10 per cent, if in total – with the addition of the principal debtor – 20 per cent have been reached. This is, I believe, a wide range of different provisions that facilitate the situation of women who are involved. They either exist or are caught in the eye. But I do not think it would be useful to completely disable the institution of guarantee or liability.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e5412	Ladies and gentlemen! Rarely has there been a legislative project in the school sector called for as many pros and contrasts as the spiritual disabled persons provided for in the Government template into the school of 10 to 14 years old. All possible concerns have been raised to me, but I am convinced also of you, such as the fact that the disabled will come under the wheels, the level is falling, it is only a fashion trend, it is to introduce the entire school, it is only about apparent equality and much more. I believe that people must not be offended if they are skeptical of the integration of mentally disabled people. I also believe that we must accept that there are prejudices that there are concerns, fears and rejections, because we are entering New Zealand when we establish such an integration. In my opinion, it is also very difficult to find the right way to achieve a meaningful and good integration, and I myself admit that I am a seeker, that I also do not know what is the right way for the disabled and also for the disabled. That is why I acknowledge being among the seekers, because I have a lot to do with the disabled: with the slightly disabled, with the severely disabled, with the most severely disabled, with the disabled. Of course, the extent to which problems are associated with full integration becomes particularly clear to you. I am, however, – I am also aware of this – looking for the right path, better said: in the search for the best way, but I am convinced that the complete integration of mind It is the right or the better way for people with disabilities to be able to form a community with non-inhibited persons and to reduce prejudices. The critics believe that the mentally disabled lives best only under the likes of His own, for what is going on around him he does not realize at all, but that is not at all true. Of course, the disabled, including the mentally disabled, builds a relationship with its environment, it has a sensitivity, and it perceives to a very large extent what is happening in its environment. And children in particular need the example of non-inhibited people. They want to participate in the topics they discuss, take note of the language, the movement, want affection and also care, and they also want to have the feeling to belong to it. I think it must be made clear that it is these feelings that it is this sensitivity in mind. Disabled people are not people who feel the most good when they are only mentally disabled again. I am convinced that those disabled people who grow up in an inclusive class community benefit greatly from the encounter, from the natural encounter with non-inhibited persons. You'll probably have gotten a lot of letters, too. I have received a letter from someone who is responsible for maintaining the special facilities and provides a positive example of an education in this special facility. They write to me – I quote: A well-known couple should make his mongoloid daughter fit for life to the best of their ability. They were advised to teach her skills in early childhood that she would need in everyday life: So the little Felicitas learned to clean up their room and to prepare small meals to a limited extent. She was very proud of these own achievements. Now, after the death of the parents, she can do little useful work in the household of relatives and is glad about it. - Quote. Perhaps the little Felicitas would be able to do not only little useful work in the case of open-minded education, so that the others can enjoy doing something so good, but in the case of a corresponding education – including integrative education – the little Felicitas would probably be able to develop interests that are important to them themselves. It would be able to find opportunities that give its development a greater space, and it would probably be able to live a more life-worthy life than to do only small useful works for others. There is nothing against it – quite the contrary: it is even to be supported – that disabled people, whether physically or mentally handicapped, also take their personal precautions, such as dressing, going cloning and being prepared so much, because this is often necessary because they do not learn it as self-evidently as those who are not disabled. I now come to speak to the non-inhibited. It is always said that the school level would suffer if they were with the disabled. I do not think that the level is suffering. First of all, we assume that there are two teachers in an integrative class, but I believe that this means that unimpeded children learn much more, because they also learn social behaviour. They gain social experience, they learn how to behave socially – and that to an extent that is not the case otherwise. Especially at a time when families are often unable to communicate this social learning, because the families themselves have been destroyed, it is extremely important that the non-inhibited, of course disabled, learn this social behaviour. The non-inhibited learn to take into account the behavior of people who behave differently from most of the people, they learn to understand for those who move worse, who can express themselves worse or what is still there, at least for those who do not find it easy to claim themselves in life. I believe that this is a good thing, that non-intrudered people are exposed to this learning process in an integrative class, because in this time when the material has such a great importance in which one believes it is best to get through with elbow technology, in which one believes that one has to cross through, it is necessary to also see the other side, to see that side where people who are significantly more difficult to carry out their tasks – and to know that there are also these pages. I believe that there will be a mutual understanding of the other position between the people with disabilities and the disabled. However, I believe that this project, which is provided for in the Government's proposal, cannot achieve the objective of good integration, and therefore I am against it despite the fundamental affirmation. The government template assumes that 20 people with disabilities and five with disabilities should sit in one class. With 25 children, however, each project only fails. I share the view with other associations and experts who believe that the recovery of the "Eselsbank" will come about if this government template becomes reality. Further prerequisites for positive integration are also appropriate teachers, a well-trained teacher – and lack of them – and a correspondingly low number of students. We have tabled a request that I will now read. Eight unimpeded and four disabled children should be in one class. That, we believe, is a prerequisite for meaningful integration. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] [[Quoted print version removed]] Ladies and gentlemen! I would ask you to take account of our amendment and not to be content to see integration in such a way that only the disabled are put into a huge class association – and accordingly trained teachers are missing.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e7133	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! In this school package, I think, there is a particular importance in the area of integration, and I think that this package must and will be a reform boost to be decided on today. Integration in school is also an important step towards social integration. In the last few days, we have been confronted with letters in which both for and against integration, especially the integration of mentally disabled people, has been mentioned. This shows that a broad discussion process has been launched. Press releases have also been made to which I will come back. From my point of view, the integration of the disabled into the school and the discussion about it has nothing to do with budget shifts, quotas, prevented elite education – as we have heard today –, exclusion, forced happiness, children second class, inn search or other martial arts, as it was unfortunately to be found in press releases of the last days. The social integration of mentally disabled people is a purely political question, and if one answers this question with yes, it is only logical that one introduces the formal framework for that which makes it possible for meaningful integration first. These conditions include the reduction of the maximum number of class students in the integration classes, advice, information and support of teachers, new forms of teaching and learning, and appropriate teaching materials, compulsory teacher training and supervision offers, and this also includes the possibility of finding the optimal way of education for a mentally disabled child who best meets his requirements. In all cases, the objective must be social integration and the creation of the conditions for all people to live a life as self-determined and independent as possible. School tests in 600 classes, 363 primary classes and 14 AHS classes have shown that the path of integration classes is a good one. There are also, of course, existing special schools, special education centres, cooperation models and support teacher systems. This is the diversity of the offer. A study on the evaluation of integration studies in primary schools clearly shows that the integration of mentally disabled people has a very positive impact on the situation in the respective classes and schools. That is very clear to all critics and to all those who are negatively opposed to this integration model! I can also give you some examples. In those schools where integration was practiced. There were more educational activities and a greater satisfaction of the parents with the school. The parents reported on greater support efforts for both weaker and more gifted students, and the children’s school joy increased. In addition, a stronger effort has been made towards near-life, cooperation and school-centred education. Finally, there was also increased cooperation and teamwork among teachers. All these are the effects I consider desirable and bring something to everyone involved in school life, both those who are disabled and those who experience school as a student or as a parent or teacher. In the next school year, about 800 primary school classes and 60 AHS subclasses should offer the integration model. This will be done on a voluntary basis, no one will be forced into anything. In my view, to make children experience "other" – under quotation marks – as early as possible is a very important teaching goal, which also takes into account § 2 SchoG, the target section of the Austrian school system. Integration from this perspective also has something to do with the involvement of other, socially weak groups. I am thinking above all of students with non-German mother tongue. Our next important political step should also be to strengthen integration efforts in this area. - Thank you.

In 1970 it was true that, as a share of gross domestic product, the Federal Government spent 2.8% on education, while the countries spent 1 percent. Today, the federal government no longer employs 2.8, but 3.9 percent of gross domestic product for education, and the countries are no longer 1 percent, but 0.6 percent. That is to say, by visiting higher schools, by expanding the middle and higher education system, there has been a massive shift in the costs of the countries to the federal government. In this case, we believe it is reasonable for countries to bear and contribute to the children of their country the relatively low cost of integration. Point Report by the Committee on Education on Amendment 149/A (E) by Mrs Maria Schaffenrath, Mr Oellingenrund Comrades on the introduction of the partial right of schools and school-book autonomy (441 of the Supplements)
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e8670	We come to item 8 of the agenda: Report by the Committee on Education on Amendment 149/A (E) by Mr Schaffenrath, Mr Ollinger and Mr Comrades on the introduction of the partial right of schools and the autonomy of schools (441 of the Supplements). There was no oral reporting. The first speaker to speak is Mrs Haller. – Ladies and gentlemen, you have the floor. A voluntary time limit of 7 minutes is indicated.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e8698	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! This debate is about a Liberal request. Although the title "Introduction of the partial right of schools and school-book autonomy" could lead to our being liberals, if we refer to the justification, to the explanations, to the purpose of this request, I must say that we must vote against it. First of all, it is true that part of this request, which concerns the inclusion of new media in the field of school books, is obsolete, and secondly, we do not support the fundamental trend of this request, because school books are to continue to be financed from the family compensation fund. As regards the situation of the Family Compensation Fund, I find a beautiful graphic from 1992 – unfortunately there is no newer, at least I do not have one – which clearly demonstrates why we cannot agree to this development. Three quarters of the benefits of this fund for real family benefits were provided in 1983; the other benefits, such as school books, free travel et cetera, were the rest. In 1992 it had already shifted so that only about two thirds of the fund for real family benefits was available. Now we have the year 1996. We have two savings packages behind us, which have worsened this situation, which has increased the share of other benefits in relation to real family benefits. I am thinking only of the increase in the share of pension funds and the corresponding pension payments in the FLAF. Although the school records have been covered, the birth allowance has been completely deleted. We are trying to get under control the financial debacle of the family compensation fund, which we have pointed out for many years, by reducing family benefits at the expense of the families by 20 percent. In this context, I would like to recall the debates of 1994 and 1995 when this threatening development was also underlined by the so-called Badelt study carried out on behalf of the Ministry of Family Affairs. This study has spoken quite vehemently of a reorganisation of this fund and, in connection with the performance of the school books from this fund, has also spoken of non-funding benefits which would be assigned to the respective department – in this case to the Ministry of Education. This would also make it easier to ensure smooth handling and genuine reform in the field of school books. I could imagine that the Minister for Education, in a general reform of the School Book Action, which we have long called for, would be easier to do with the contents of the School Books if the entire competence in their field were to be. We are therefore in favour of a fundamental reform and not just an extension of autonomy. Unfortunately, the efforts of an interministerial working group to reform this fund have been in the sand, and the long-standing promises of a total of three family ministers have been dissolved, as have so many others. The current Minister of Family Bartenstein is more likely to give me the impression that he is still ready to make the buffer for financial policy – in order to use an expression from the Badelt study again – and that he is more likely to help re-use the FLAF's projected surpluses for 1997. From these fundamental considerations, we cannot accept this liberal request, because we believe that this request is merely a move towards the efforts currently under way in the field of FLAF.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e8743	Mrs Moser is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Please, ladies and gentlemen. Voluntary speaking time limit: 5 minutes.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e8764	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The surplus of FLAF projected by you, Mrs Haller, for 1997 would be beautiful, but unfortunately it will not exist, not for 1997 and not for 1998. On the subject, I would like to say: Part-righteousness is the ultimate goal, but the way there is certainly about deregulation as part of the autonomy for schools. The schools have so far been largely monocatically organized. In contrast, the universities have a centuries-old tradition of self-organisation and administration, which is why they also have extensive experience and beyond a marked, partly certainly already bloated, administrative apparatus. For the partial rights of schools with different sizes and different human resources, various solutions may have to be created or made possible. How did this autonomy start for schools? – When it was said in January 1993 that from autumn 1993 the Austrian schools were undergoing major changes in the house, there was joy and excitement about more mobility and decentralised development at schools, but unfortunately God’s fear and uncertainty as well. Schools should be given further opportunities in the educational design of the hourboard. Freer hand than before, the legislator also conced the schools in terms of opening and division numbers. The decisions on this were now taken by the school-partnership bodies, i.e. at the school- and class-forums, with a two-thirds majority to be reached for all decisions. ==References====External links== SchoG-Novelle for the AHS eight hours and for the primary school 16 hours for free disposition. The second step then moved towards the transfer of financial resources. Fear and euphoria again seized: How to meet the extended scope? – Although the school tests at the primary school had brought good experience, they would rather fear themselves first! School autonomy – actually, but how? A congress on school autonomy took place and brought about the reform pedagogical renewal shift. Dr. Bernd Schilcher, the committed president of the Styrian National School Council, presented a welcome reference work. For the 1995/96 school year, 24 model schools were given larger spaces in educational, financial and organisational terms. A company consulting firm supports schools with modern management methods. Some Vienna AHS directors, however, vehemently opposed the financial autonomy of schools to the Wehr. The concern about extra burden and, above all, concern about budgeting found their echo in the media. The Vienna City School Council President even believed that schools could no longer be heated, especially those supplied with district heating. Wiener Freunde told me that district heating was more future-oriented and cost-effective, but perhaps he thought that the schools would have to pay a more expensive price and the municipal buildings would have to pay a cheaper price. Most educators, however, demanded and continued to follow the path of autonomy. In April 1996, 64 percent of primary schools and 43 percent of AHS had set up school autonomous measures. The opening up to the outside was very positive, especially with regard to cooperation with the local economy, the increased emphasis on cross-border forms of education and the active participation of teachers and parents in school development. For 1996/97 there is now even a model country school council, and schools can take funds by renting premises or by sponsors and also spend them independently. Economic thinking and action is moving into schools! To the school's autonomy, there's so much to say: The free schoolbook is well known to have been installed on the ideological basis of the right to education, because no one should be prevented from obtaining good schooling by a poorer family income. No child should be prevented from using the best schoolbooks through the poverty of his parents. But our society is not infinitely rich, and a certain principle of reuse must simply be possible! I'm up-to-date, but not every year. The Iron Curtain has only fallen once, and Armstrong and Aldrin have landed on the moon only once. No one can call into question the sensible outsourcing and reuse. In the course of the school's autonomy, it was thus possible for the school year 1997/98 to use 5 percent of the school's budget for learning games and automation-aided data mediums. In the following school year, even 10 percent of the acquisition of other learning resources is possible. I can conclude with the words of Mrs Gehrer: I think it is particularly important to bring joy to the students, to show commitment to making them tasteful throughout life, to give them a certain degree of solidarity and community sense, to demand benefits, but to be aware that they cannot always be measured in terms of material values. – In this sense, I still wish our Federal Minister good nerves for the sensitivity and sense of the right path!

Only in this we could recognize essential steps towards autonomy. Ladies and gentlemen! One must not only want autonomy, but also really allow! We therefore ask you to support our request.

Of course, entry into partial autonomy could only take place in such a way that the resources are managed independently by the schools. I think it does not divide us too much in the matter, except perhaps that some of you have the hope of being able to "get rid of" the problem of schoolbook action in general on this path. I do not hope that we are going in this direction, and from this point of view we can also understand our request.

Autonomy promotes the commitment and willingness of teachers. Autonomy also activates parents and students to participate in school together and to decide. However, there are also investigations from practice that demonstrate how much school autonomy is proven. The Minister of Education has had the Centre for School Development examined how school autonomy is adopted. This study showed that three-quarters of all higher schools, two-thirds of the primary schools and about one-third of the AHS already use autonomous design possibilities in their schools. At some teaching institutions, the autonomy – and that is also to be said – as well as manual conflicts in the teacher's college. But I do not think it should be overrated, because – and I agree with the opinion of the Federal Minister – school autonomy is nothing final, nothing is finished, but school autonomy is a discussion and development process. The autonomy study also shows that about half of the school projects were initiated by the teachers themselves and about 30 to 35 percent by the school management. What I think will have a particularly motivating effect on schools is financial self-design. So far, economical schools have been "punished" if they have not spent money. These funds were returned to the Minister of Finance. In the future, this is different; the schools can use these so-called saved funds optimally according to their credit. This promotes independence and, above all, independent thinking among teachers. This is something that we will urgently need in the future, considering how quickly the situation in the economy, in politics and in social cohesion will change. We need flexible, team-fit and socially competent workers, and this requires these flexible school facilities. Thank you at this point. The People's Party will not vote in favour of this request by Mrs Schaffenrath, Mr Oellinger and Mr Comrades.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e9546	To answer this urgent question, the Federal Chancellor has spoken. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e9567	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! One thing I would like to make clear right at the beginning of my reply: What individual politicians who are not members of the Federal Government say at home or abroad: The policy of the Austrian Federal Government regarding nuclear energy is unchanged and clear: We do not want nuclear power plants, because the risks associated with operation and waste disposal are still not solved and – I assume – cannot be solved either. We are also convinced that, if nuclear energy is followed by cost-truth principles and therefore involves the decommissioning costs and storage costs of nuclear power, nuclear energy is by no means economic than other forms of energy. We represent this position with our immediate neighbours, and we, of course, continue to represent this position as a government at the level of the European Union and related organisations. I therefore say this with such deep conviction, because I myself have defined the course of the Federal Government on this issue, so that I can assure you: There will be no change in the line against nuclear energy in the Federal Government. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In accordance with the principle of separation of power, I should like to ask the authors to ask the Members of the People's Party in the European Parliament, who voted against the request, directly what they thought in their voting habits. But I admit that I too thought about it when I first heard of it, because this voting behaviour of the said Members was so different from what they had just explained during their own campaign in the final election campaign. But, of course, policy on nuclear energy is being made in the Federal Government. Of course, our position – as already explained – has not changed. However, I take Mr Barmüller's statement and the question of the occasion for a number of fundamental comments on this subject, especially as the interpretations of the success or failure of this project differ widely. An important measure of determination is the time factor. I would remind you, ladies and gentlemen, that we always had a medium-term planning horizon in mind and that the claim was not to be able to bring about a decommissioning or prevention of nuclear power stations close to the border today or tomorrow. However, even if this "prevention" should be used as a scale, it must be stated with great clarity that the Western Community, under the leadership of the USA, also the European Commission or the G7, with its strategy of achieving an early decommissioning of particularly dangerous installations by means of massive Western financial assistance, has not been successful. The controversy surrounding the Kosloduj nuclear power plant in Bulgaria in autumn 1995 may only be remembered as a particularly striking example. Another measure of success control is whether the Austrian commitment has increased the safety status of neighbouring nuclear power plants. The answer is yes and is supported by the example of the Slovak Bohunice AKV, in which since our intervention in the wake of an Austrian committee of experts and personal negotiations with the respective Prime Ministers since 1990, the probability of an accident has been reduced by the factor 5 to 10. Also at the Slovenian nuclear power plant Krško, an international group of experts based on an Austrian initiative has put their finger on the wounds and thus the basis for the almost completed rehabilitation measures. Thus, it remained to measure the Austrian nuclear energy policy in terms of whether it has fulfilled its claim to take up a step-by-step role. In this respect, however, no complaint has yet been voiced by the sharpest critics of the Austrian nuclear energy policy. Depending on the point of view, Austria is a source of hope and orientation for nuclear-critical people in the country and abroad, as well as an uncomfortable but professionally respected negotiating partner. In addition, Austria has highlighted some international discussions and has contributed significantly to the fact that international financial institutions, first and foremost the European Bank for Reconstruction and Development, will take special care in dealing with project applications for nuclear installations in the future. Some observers also believe that Austria's commitment in the case of the Mochovce nuclear power plant has in the long term stopped the World Bank's entry into the financing of nuclear projects. High house! Although nearly two years of membership of the European Union was a relatively short period, a provisional balance was nevertheless sought. First of all, we must be aware that we have been clearly guided by the ambitious nature of our concerns. The creation of "critical" coalitions also with states, which largely share the Austrian intentions, has proved to be extremely difficult and complex. Moreover, as a new member and as a small state, Austria is in a more unfavourable position compared to large Member States. However, it appears to be justified – and I note this once again – that Austria has acquired substantial recognition as a professional and consistent negotiating partner. This does not mean, of course, that Austria has been able to implement its positions by a large majority, since other Member States do not hesitate to use the instruments available to them to implement their interests with due emphasis. Nevertheless, Austria has been able to influence so many discussions and decisions on a sustainable basis at European level. It mentions, without weighting, the adoption of the new TACIS Regulation, the consideration of nuclear safety as an essential aspect of the pre- and accession strategies for the Central and Eastern European countries, the discussion on the nature and extent of the European Union's participation in KEDO, the discussion on Community nuclear research, both in terms of the planned expansion of the 4th Framework Programme. Framework programmes for research and technological development as well as for the design of the 5th Framework Programme. Framework programmes, the field of technical assistance in the nuclear sector, in particular in the framework of PHARE and TACIS, and in particular the impact of Austrian activities in connection with the planned financing of the completion of the Mochovce nuclear power station. In this particular case, Austria was called upon, as a "newling" – yet without a deep knowledge of the informal structures and decision-making processes of the European Union – to give its position adequate hearing. This has probably been achieved in an unexpectedly high degree. The individual questions, ladies and gentlemen, I reply as follows: Questions 1 and 2: In my capacity as Federal Chancellor of the Republic, I have made the fight against nuclear power in Europe – both in the West and in the East – a personal concern since the beginning of my office. Although the access to this subject and the commitment it is pursued require from our coalition partner of a "reheat phase" – I am imagining a little flattering comment on the Washington trip of university professor Dr. Heindler regarding the Temelin AKW by then Vice-Cancer Dr. Busek – today I have no doubt about the uniformity of the government line. [[Rejoicing with the Freemans]] In its explanatory statement, the applicants themselves pointed out the joint decisions and wishes of the Federal Government, from the Government Convention to the Energy Report. As I have already said, I see no sign that the coalition partner wishes to remove himself from this consensus. Question 3: I am convinced that this credibility has been given to a great extent in view of the various activities of the Federal Government and that the voting behaviour of those Members to the European Parliament, which, I stress again, has not been cancelled in the exercise of their free mandate. Question 4: First of all, the Austrian proposal, in the sense of an environmentally sound development, as referred to in Article 2 of the EC Treaty, should also include the long-term exit from the energy use of nuclear energy as a target in Community law, which is still the subject of discussions at the Intergovernmental Conference. It is however, and I have done so repeatedly, to point out that the implementation of an amendment, resolution or replacement of the EURATOM Treaty requires the approval of all the Member States of the European Union. Without prejudice to this, we will, of course, continue to act consistently within the meaning of our fundamental objectives. Question 5: In the first place, it is not about amending the EURATOM Treaty or, as some believe, abolishing it equally completely, but about representing Austrian interests in the practical everyday life of the European Union, i.e. in the implementation of existing Community law. I have already given examples in my general remarks of the fact that Austria is already repressing its position on the principle of nuclear energy policy in all the areas of integration in question. These examples have also shown that we have not been unsuccessful in this regard. The amendment of the EURATOM Treaty itself is only possible within the framework of an Intergovernmental Conference of the European Union. In conclusion, the Federal Government will also be guided by these considerations in the preparation of the Austrian Presidency.

In November 1996, the Commission negotiated and adopted a majority vote. Article 15a of the Treaty, which already entered into force on 13 December 1990. On April 1, 1978, Maurer, Leopold Gratz and Theodor Kery used as a planning community in East. Four environmental ministers, together with the state politicians of Lower Austria and Vienna, have put their political weight in the scales to give the planning team appropriate back cover. The concept for the Donau-Auen National Park was created with a lot of expertise and personal use, although overwhelming interests and often unfulfilled requirements made planning work an almost hopeless obstacle course. The company Marchfeldkanal has done good work, which we warmly congratulate and thank you. The Federal Government, represented by the Federal Government, and the countries of Lower Austria and Vienna, each represented by the head of state, have agreed to establish and maintain a national park in the area of Danube Auen in and east of Vienna, while safeguarding the function of the Danube as an international waterway and safeguarding groundwater resources for drinking water supply. The management of the Danube-Auen National Park is carried out by the National Park Society, in which the federal government is involved with 50 percent and the countries of Lower Austria and Vienna with 25 percent each. In order to carry out the tasks assigned to it, the National Park Society will have to draw up an economic and financial plan for the coming year annually and continue until 30. Each year, each year, a financial statement and an annual report shall be presented, administrative transactions shall be conducted, compensation shall be determined, user losses and economic difficulties arising from the acquisition of the property shall be determined. In addition, the National Park Society has up to 30. Each April of each year, a report on the implementation of the measures provided for in the current annual programme and their costs. A scientific advisory committee will be set up to advise the administration on the subject of spatial and landscape planning, botany, zoology, limeology and water management. In the first year of operation, i.e. 1997, the National Park covers over 9,300 hectares for the first time. After the addition of additional areas on a voluntary basis, the area can grow to 11,500 meters in the extension phase. The current costs will be around 40 million schilling in 1997. In the final development, the costs per year will increase to around 60 million schilling. This includes the costs of the National Park Society, the physical and personnel costs for natural space management by the Federal Forest as well as the forestry management of the municipality of Vienna. It must also be noted that the right of the property owners to compensation for loss of income and loss of use is indivisible for us. The same applies to the Federal Forest and the municipality of Vienna. Natural space management means a National Park-compliant care of hunting, fishing as well as agriculture and forestry. Dear ladies and gentlemen! The Article 15a Treaty between the Federal Government and the countries of Lower Austria and Vienna regulates the financing and the boundaries of the park and initiates the implementation of the project. Even massive opponents have not been able to identify material shortcomings in this concept. The polemics in the arguments of the opponents confirms the high quality of the concept. I particularly welcome the fact that, in the last debate on 30 November, the Green Group in the person of Monika Langthaler was also in favour of the Group of the Greens. In October, the Article 15a Treaty was considered to be positive and its approval was signaled. Now everything has to be done to gain the trust of the population in this region and to raise awareness of nature in people. One of the most beautiful natural jewels in our country is thus protected and preserved. Our group will therefore be happy to agree to this Treaty. - Thank you.

With its 9 300 hectares of outdoor space, this national park is surrounded by one of the most productive areas of Austria and one of the most valuable agricultural areas we have at all: In the north it is the Marchfeld, the corn chamber of Austria, in the south the Vienna Basin, the garden of Austria. Of course, groundwater does not know any defined boundaries, even at the national park, it is only in accordance with the physical laws. It is therefore of enormous importance what is happening in the environment, what is happening in the entire catchment area of this national park. Since Austria's accession to the EU, there have been tremendous and significant improvements in the management of the OPUL project. I can quote them here: For 90 percent of the domestic agricultural area, intensive farming has been excluded. We have introduced a variety of fruits to 75 percent of cereals and maize. It takes place on a total of 245,000 hectares of autumn and winter greening – and now the number is all over Austria. In addition, there is – and this is crucial – a drop in trade fertilizer expenditure on nitrogen by minus 8 percent, with phosphorus fertilisation by minus 12 percent and potassium by minus 20 percent. The decline is also enormous in the use of plant protection products. I'm just calling a number: The growth regulators have a minimum of 58 percent. During the same period there has been an increase in organic farming by 37 percent. Why do I say that? – Because this change in management has enormous effects on the groundwater of the Marchfeld and also of the Vienna Basin, and the sum of these measures will bring a significant improvement in the quality of groundwater in combination with the national park protection provisions. When it comes to water, one must not first and foremost have in mind the quality assurance. Our objective must be to maintain groundwater in the national park on a quality water basis. I can say: Austria cannot have enough reserves on high-quality drinking water. From this point of view, the combination of national park and groundwater protection area is a very useful solution. Perhaps our children and our grandchildren will be happy about this decision again.

In spite of this, exceptions were reached during the Austrian accession negotiations – with a transitional period of four years – in the labelling, classification and prohibition. During this period, the Austrian provisions will be examined by the European Union with the aim of either incorporating the Austrian provisions, as we hope, into European law or adapting the Austrian provisions to EU directives. The main objective of this amendment was to simplify existing rules, which, as I believe, could also be achieved substantially. For example, the law on detergents, as we heard before, is integrated into the law. The amendment also provides for a number of regulatory powers, such as to ensure environmental compatibility and registration. Twelve existing regulations could thus be combined into a single one. It seems particularly important to me that this also simplifies the toxic part in several points and improves in terms of protection objectives, that the full recognition of Austria's notifications in Europe is ensured that the legal basis for Austria's participation in the EU's Altstoffprüfungsprogramm has been established, that the implementing authorities are available to implement rules, and that a database for toxic information centres is established at the Federal Minister for Health. It is also to be welcomed in the sense of a practice-oriented administration that the Ministry of the Environment intends to replace the variety of lists so far with a single comprehensive, electronically available list. In conclusion: I believe that this novel is a step towards a modern EU-compliant chemical right, but it will have to be followed until full conformity is achieved. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e15736	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In my speech, allow me to address certain amendments to the Environmental Impact Assessment Act. I would like to predict that I am in principle in favour of a change in the EIA legislation, but I find some passages problematic. From its conception, the EIA law is more suitable for pulsating installations, so it is intended to amend the provisions of the EIA legislation for infrastructure projects such as road and rail. The construction of shorter sections of the road with a length of less than five kilometres should no longer be necessary in the future if a sea height of 1,200 metres is not exceeded and an additional traffic load of more than 20 percent is not expected from the stock, such as railway underpasses, bridges, small distances, local crossings and crossings, an environmental impact assessment. This regulation will take a step in a worrying direction, as the EIA law has been created to verify whether construction projects are environmentally compatible or not. It is therefore not relevant, in my opinion, how large or how small a construction project is, because environmental compatibility has nothing to do with the size of the project. I am not opposed to sensible small cost-effective driveways and the construction of shorter sections of the road. They contribute, if they do not select monster projects, to the reduction of traffic and to the safety of the population. However, they must not be built in such a way as to facilitate or simplify transit traffic. In East Tyrol, we have a "good" example, as I do not think it should be, namely the bypass of waste. ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e18485	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! The current legislative novel on federal procurement law and foreign employment law 1975 has therefore become necessary – and this has already been done by my predecessor – because we need to adapt our national law to EU law. It does not regulate the invitation to tender as such, but the procurement procedures which are clearly normed and laid down therein. Of course, this comprehensive lawmaking work has required appropriate preliminary advice. It's been working on this for weeks. It has really worked very intensively on the subcommittee. This is reason enough for us – this should be emphasized – to express our recognition to the committed employees, both from the government agencies and from the interest groups. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I have already said that the novelle became necessary because we have to adapt our legal status to the EC legal system. It is crucial that there are thresholds in the new law. That is, everything that is about a threshold, which is precisely standardised here, results in an EU-wide call for tender, and there is also a notification and justification requirement for the supplier when it is being processed. That's something very essential. In the Committee's deliberations, we have also succeeded in establishing the obligation to notify under the thresholds in the law and in giving the justification on request. The awards under the thresholds continue to take place after the ÖNORM 2050, a standardisation which is known to us, I think, all over the world. In the context of the Foreign Employment Act, it is important to note that the new law provides that the bidder no longer has to prove that he had or had no problems with the Foreign Employment Act, but the client. This brings better conditions and less bureaucracy for our economy, and that is what we have valued. That is, I believe, an advantage for all of us, but especially for our economy. Furthermore, it is established that the Federal Government can also, by regulation, order the legal protection of the Federal Procurement Act for contracts below the thresholds. It was important to us – that was a very long and intense discussion – to standardize § 43a to the effect that, when leaving, the awarding body must immediately make this communication. Thus, the notifier can make his further decisions without delay and also benefit from the legal instruments provided for in the law. This is new land, but it is a very important improvement for the bidder in particular. Furthermore, the improvement of legal protection for the bidder with a suspensive effect is also included in the novelle. I would just like to point out that § 66 has all been very busy with all of us, namely the prohibition of discrimination. We have solved the problem in such a way that we have noted in a committee that the selection of competitors is limited to those who have special professional qualifications, in so far as this is justified. I think this federal award law is a very modern law very effective for our economy. This is also the reason why we have worked hard and will now also give our assent.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e18548	The next speaker was Mr Krüger. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e18569	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I don't think I'm going to hurt anyone if I get very short in view of the advanced period. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Dr. Hazelsteiner.]] Thank you for the intermediate applause. It has already been mentioned by the two previous speakers that this matter of law is a consensual matter. We liberals have commented on criticism points in the Constitutional Committee, but at the same time we have shown that we do not question consensus here and will vote in third reading. Let me comment briefly on the modification of the Foreign Employment Act in 1975. The purpose of this legislative amendment is to penalise the activity of black workers, i.e. foreign skilled workers and workers who do not have employment permits, in such a way that applicants or entrepreneurs are temporarily excluded from public contracts in the event of an appropriate legal knowledge. However, ladies and gentlemen, I do not believe that this objective, which I have just mentioned, is achieved by the provisions of the amended Foreign Employment Act. For what reason? First of all, § 28b Abs. 1 of the innovative Foreign Employment Act provides that the Federal Minister for Labour and Social Affairs has to provide information on whether a particular entrepreneur who participates in a tender or receives the award has committed a substantial breach of this Federal Act. This is the first time that the black sheep are covered by the businessmen, who are not even asked by the contracting authority for their seriality in respect of compliance with this law. In order to help break through the objective of the law, it would be necessary to introduce a reversal, namely the obligation for contracting authorities to obtain an inquiry from the Federal Minister for Labour and Social Affairs in the tendering of public contracts, instead of leaving it to the contracting authorities whether an information is collected or not. Furthermore, there is a lack of consequence in the fact that the period of eradication is already after a year when an entrepreneur has failed to comply with the provisions of the Foreign Employment Act twice. If he has only failed to do so once, this has no effect on him at all, then he does not come on the black list. The fact that it is able to violate the provisions of the Ausländergesetz, once in a completely non-punitive manner, insists on the comparison with the "freedom" of the ABGB's general principles of compensation for damage relating to the liability of animal owners. In other words, He is allowed to violate and has no fear of being punished at all. If he violates another time, then there is a substantial injury, then he is excluded. After a year, the eradication is done again, and he's back in the game. Ladies and gentlemen! I conclude by saying: We liberals will agree to the Government's proposal, but believe that the half-hearted amendment to the Ausländerbehörde's employment law does not reduce, let alone be eliminated.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e18876	We'll get to the 21st. Agenda item. The applicant is also given the floor. Please, Mrs Haller.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e18900	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! This is a request made by the so-called part-time offensive of the Federal Government. It would be a signal that has long been set in Germany and in other European countries, an offensive that should also be a signal to the economy to create more qualified part-time work opportunities. This request is not new, and I can therefore keep my comments brief. The content is known to the plenary and also to the Secretary of State. I have already described in detail the benefits of the arrangements provided for in the request several times. It's the third time I brought him in. I was very pleased to see Mr Schlögl at the last edition of this request – he had the number 104 in the XX. legislative period and was rejected – the content of which was very positive. Finally, the rejection of this liberal request has led the Constitutional Committee to have a joint request from the government parties that by the end of the year there should be a presentation on this subject here in the plenary session. Mr State Secretary! If you have followed the media reports, you know that you are trying – I am very happy to agree and very much agree to this – to keep this promise. I know that you have already presented a very useful draft, which can also be used as a form of free movement. There has also been a public debate in the media about a month ago, but now it has become completely silent again, because the GÖD, the trade union public service, is blocking this attitude. The trade union of the public service is seeking rights of intervention in the possibility of part-time work, it is intended to have these rights of intervention laid down in the law, which is intended to make it possible for the trade union to determine that every post of leave must be re-occupied. It thus presents a bad witness to the company State. It does not provide him with freedom of choice – it could be that in the field of civil servants it is obvious that some jobs are actually affordable. The fact is that qualified, good part-time jobs are needed throughout the EU. This has confirmed a new study. And in the previous, very intense debate on the equal treatment of women, Mrs Brinek of the ÖVP said that 56 percent of women – there is also a study – really want something to happen in this area, that there is more of these skilled part-time jobs. That was always a socialist side-by-side approach. Now, for me, the question is: Who rules in Austria? – Parliament is not the way you see it. The Secretary of State does not seem to be the case either. It is the union of the public service that has the say! To this end, I can only say the following: A worker's representative, who is merely in favour of the fact that new jobs cannot be created, has, I believe, really lost its sense. If this behaviour, which we have so far had in Austria and which has led to a very serious change in the political situation in Austria, is still possible, I can only say - even in view of the advanced period - that: Good night, Austria!
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e18945	President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats), who have spoken on behalf of the Group of the European People's I'll give it to her.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-11-28-020-XX-NRSITZ-00048_d7e18967	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! Mrs. Haller! The FPÖ's request of course also expresses a legitimate concern of public servants, namely the desire for a real part-time scheme, something which is not possible at the moment. But, Mrs Haller, if you say that jobs are affordable, that means more unemployed. That must be clear to us, too. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Without wishing to go ahead with the negotiations between the social partners – and still the subject of the social partners’ negotiations – your proposal is now obsolete, because the subject of the negotiations has been a reform package, a package for the more flexible design of working time in the public service. I would like to say here that it is not a deterioration in the public sector, nor is it a regulation on the part of workers. In recent years, public sector workers have had numerous savings. The most important project that the social partners have – on the one hand the Public Service Trade Union and on the other hand the State Secretary for Official Affairs – is the achievement of real part-time work. And that is, as I said, a legitimate concern, because at present the reduction of the service period is only 50 percent possible, but not 60, 70 and 80 percent, and only in the care of a young child or a family. It is therefore necessary to reorganise part-time employment. Part-time employment should be possible for any reason. The extent should be made more flexible. The duration will be increased from currently four years to ten years, and early termination of the reduction in the service period will also be possible at the request of the official. An important point is the organisation of working time for the employee. Another important point is the creation of new jobs through part-time schemes, given the number of unemployed. As part of the working time package, however, the free year or Sabbath is also discussed – we can read this in the media. A service legal framework is to be set up, and there will be extended part-time models – we know that from the newspaper –: five years of work: four years of full-time with 80 percent of the reference, one year free of charge with also 80 percent of the reference, but other percentages and free frameworks are also imaginable. The crucial moment in labour market policy, however, is clearly the need to re-occupy planning centres which are free for one year. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] However, there are still questions to be resolved, namely the safeguarding of replacement workers, the recruitment of replacement workers and the participation of staff representatives in the recruitment of temporary staff. The implementation of this part-time model, which is undoubtedly a major one, constitutes a major project and calls for a radical extension of civil service law. However, it must be clear: not exclusively to the detriment of the staff. The free year is also of enormous importance to education policy and has a positive impact on the labour market through the opportunity to re-acquire qualifications. The other projects included in this package are related to the implementation of the EU Working Time Directives, which provide for a weekly working time framework of 48 hours for the public service. From today's point of view, I can only say that the general introduction of calculation periods of one year, i.e. year-by-year, is not possible, even in the field of executives. This is to be considered for facilities with very irregular but calculable work attacks, such as in agriculture and forestry, in the federal farms and, in some cases, in sub-areas with strong seasonal fluctuations, such as in federal sports homes. However, ladies and gentlemen, it is important to ensure that this does not result in an overtime reduction without compensation. I have outlined the extent of the reforms that go far beyond what the F proposal proposes. All the ideas that are now being discussed among the social partners will, of course, be the subject of negotiations here in the House. So the F application is already running open doors. That is why he cannot expect the approval of my group.
